### 4. Decisions and questions asked of Taylor

#### 4.1 Class decisions (main pass, before the canvass)
Rows cite these as "OR gaps log §4 class decision N". Each states its statute in the statute's own words, every quotation checked against the corpus (44 quotations, 0 misses).
1. Scope: ORS 90.100 to 90.465 for ordinary tenancies; facility tenancies (ORS 90.505 to 90.850, 'a manufactured dwelling park or a marina' (ORS 90.100(16))) and the facility submetering rules (ORS 90.560 to 90.584) are named as a boundary, not drafted for; the ORS 90.110 exclusions (transient, vacation and employee occupancy; institutional residence except off-campus nondormitory housing) are stated where a topic turns on them.
2. The waiver bar (ORS 90.245(1)(a)-(d)), its unenforceability and knowing-use penalty of up to three months' periodic rent (ORS 90.245(2)), and the one statutory exception, informal dispute resolution (ORS 90.220(5)), which excludes 'mandatory mediation or arbitration' (ORS 90.100(24)). A waiver topic states the default rule first.
3. Habitability 'at all times during the tenancy' (ORS 90.320(1)); no receipt of rent free of it (ORS 90.250); chores only in a writing meeting all three conditions of ORS 90.320(2)(a)-(c).
4. Fees: the closed list and the written-agreement requirement (ORS 90.302(1)); 'Fee' means a nonrefundable payment (ORS 90.100(17)); no liquidated damages except the early-termination fee (ORS 90.302(5)); the twice-damages-or-$300 penalty (ORS 90.302(8)); the (7) exclusions; fees are not rent (ORS 90.302(6)).
5. One fault wording for every new charge clause, taken from ORS 90.325(2)(b) and (3); the tenant-remedy fault bars (ORS 90.360(4); ORS 90.365(6); ORS 90.368(5)(d)) are quoted from their own sections, never merged.
6. The remedy sections (ORS 90.360, 90.365, 90.368) and the mitigation duty (ORS 90.125(1)).
7. Access is ORS 90.322 only, including the separate-writing showing agreement (ORS 90.322(1)(d)).
8. Rules: ORS 90.262(1)-(2), as amended by Or. Laws 2026, ch. 60, § 1.
9. Fixed terms can't be changed unilaterally (ORS 90.220(2)); the month-to-month default (ORS 90.220(7)(b)); ORS 90.427 read whole before stating for-cause or conversion rules.
10. Currency (§1.3), including the ORS 90.100 renumbering from (52) on by Or. Laws 2026, ch. 23, § 4 (both numbers given) and the dated versions.
11. One boundary sentence for an absence, naming exactly what was searched.
12. Part 5's NEEDS_REVIEW policy: a point only case law could settle is stated with the cautious course and the row stays VERIFIED.
13. Local ordinances named, not researched; ORS 91.225 preemption pointed to.
14. Victims: ORS 90.453, 90.456, 90.459, 90.449 and ORS 105.128, pointed to through the existing rows.
15. Confidential information: Or. Laws 2026, ch. 61, § 2 (uncompiled).
16. Answered-elsewhere topics (gaps item 2) get a short education row keyed to their own topic that states Oregon's rule and points to the carrying row.
17. Tag first, with the rule 26 basis check both ways and the fee and exculpation screens recorded in the tag segment.
18. At most one OR row of each content type per topic, tags included.
19. A declined lawful option gets an education row.
20. Quote convention (single quotes for statute or rule text only, each followed by its citation).
21. Citation format (the prompt's).

#### 4.1a Questions asked of Taylor
None. Every judgment call was decided in the pass and recorded as a decision (§4.2); no point needed Taylor (rule 76).

#### 4.2 Decisions recorded by topic

- **snow-removal**: No new Oregon lease clause for `snow-removal` or `landscaping-irrigation`, and no tag of the shared clauses: Oregon lets a tenant take on these tasks only under ORS 90.320(2), whose (2)(c) requires 'adequate consideration for the agreement is specifically stated' (ORS 90.320(2)(c)); the shared clauses state no consideration, and the multi-state variants that say the tasks are "part of the bargain reflected in the Monthly Rent" do not state a specific consideration either (Claude's reading). `tenant-maintenance-tasks-or` (topic `tenant-repair-agreement`) already carries a compliant clause with snow and yard examples, so a second clause on each topic would duplicate it; the education rows `edu-snow-removal-or` and `edu-landscaping-irrigation-or` point to it. If the main pass prefers a topic-keyed clause, the wording would be `tenant-maintenance-tasks-or` narrowed to the one task.
- **landscaping-irrigation**: Correction to the decision filed under `snow-removal`: the clauses that say the tasks are "part of the bargain reflected in the Monthly Rent" are the single-state variants `landscaping-irrigation-ri` and `snow-removal-ri`, not multi-state rows; they cannot be tagged in any case because each cites its own state (OR gaps log §4 class decision 17). The multi-state `landscaping-irrigation` and `snow-removal` clauses state no consideration at all, which ORS 90.320(2)(c) requires. The outcome is unchanged: no tag and no new clause; `tenant-maintenance-tasks-or` carries the compliant clause.
- **designated-repairer**: Added an optional Oregon clause `designated-repairer-or` (not a default), because ORS 90.368(4)(b) lets a landlord specify the repair people for the minor-defect remedy on two conditions ('reasonable' and not diminishing the tenant's rights) and twenty-odd states already carry an optional designated-repairer clause; the 2026-10-04 pass answered the topic only by pointer and recorded no OR log §6.1 decision on it. Drafting calls: the clause is confined to ORS 90.368 (no designation right exists for ORS 90.365 essential services); it adds a fallback (another qualified repairer when none listed is available within a reasonable time for no more than the deductible amount) so the list cannot diminish the remedy; it uses only the existing variable {{landlord_repair_contact}}; basis SERVES_LANDLORD, matching the other states' designated-repairer clauses. If the main pass prefers education only, drop the clause; the education row stands alone.
- **governmental-fines**: No Oregon lease clause on government fines, and the shared optional clause tenant-caused-fines-as (17 states) is not tagged OR. Reasons: (1) it is a reimbursement term ("Tenant will reimburse Landlord the amount Landlord paid"), which in Oregon reads as a charge outside the closed fee list of ORS 90.302(1) unless framed as damages; (2) its fault test ("caused or permitted") is not the class decision 5 wording required for any OR clause charging the tenant (ORS 90.325(2)(b), (3)); (3) it carries a "[number of days]" bracket placeholder; (4) Oregon already lets the landlord recover a fine the tenant's breach caused as damages under ORS 90.401(2) without a clause, as hoa-compliance-or does for association fines. If the main pass wants an Oregon optional clause, it would recover "as damages for that breach" the actual amount of a fine caused by damage or conduct described in the class decision 5 wording, exclude fines for the landlord's own duties and any charge from a call to police or emergency services, and require a copy of the citation; I did not draft one because the edu row's damages route covers it and a new clause is a product call.
- **government-fee-reimbursement**: Shared clause government-fee-reimbursement-ky (10 states) is not tagged OR and no Oregon clause is drafted. The shared clause has the tenant reimburse "a fee pertaining to the property, such as a rental registration or licensing fee". In Oregon a local-government charge reaches the tenant only as a public service charge under ORS 90.315(4)(a), and that term excludes 'business license fees or dwelling inspection fees' (ORS 90.315(1)(b)(B)); a stand-alone reimbursement of a registration or licensing fee would be a charge outside the closed fee list of ORS 90.302(1), with the ORS 90.302(8) penalty. Treating rental registration and licensing fees as within the exclusion is my reading, stated as such in edu-government-fee-reimbursement-or. Public service charges that do qualify are already billable through utility-billing-or (ORS 90.315(1)(d) counts 'public services' as a utility or service), so no new clause is needed. Also, the shared clause uses "[all / the following share of]" and "[number]" bracket placeholders.
- **rent-escalation**: No Oregon rent-escalation lease clause drafted, and the shared escalation clauses (for example rent-escalation-mp, rent-increase-midterm-ky) are not tagged. An Oregon clause could lawfully schedule an increase in a fixed term after the first year (it is agreed, so ORS 90.220(2) is not offended), but on my reading ORS 90.323(2)(b) still requires a separate written notice at least 90 days before each increase, with the ORS 90.323(3) contents, and the amount is capped by ORS 90.323(2)(d) unless ORS 90.323(5) applies. A clause would therefore have to say that each scheduled increase takes effect only on a compliant notice and never above the statutory maximum, which makes it little more than a restatement. The education row edu-rent-escalation-or states the rule. If the product wants an optional Oregon clause, it should be CONSTRAINED_TERM, apply only to fixed terms over one year, use no new variable unless one is listed, and carry the notice and cap conditions in its text.
- **guarantor-renewal**: Drafted an Oregon optional guaranty clause (guaranty-or) instead of tagging the shared guaranty-as (22 states). Reason: ORS 41.580(1) requires the memorandum of a promise to answer for another's debt to be in writing, subscribed by the party charged and 'expressing the consideration'; guaranty-as recites no consideration, so on Oregon's text it may not satisfy the statute of frauds (class decision 17: tag only where the row states Oregon's law as written). guaranty-or copies guaranty-as with a consideration recital, uses the existing variables {{guarantor_name}} and {{guaranty_max_liability}} in place of the bracket placeholders, and fixes the coverage at renewals, extensions and month-to-month continuation because ORS 90.427(4)(c) converts a post-first-year fixed term to month-to-month automatically. If the main pass prefers no Oregon guaranty clause (OR log §18.2 recorded the topic as confirmed absent and offered none), withdraw guaranty-or and drop the sentence in edu-guarantor-renewal-or that points to it.
- **dv-deposit-timing**: ORS 90.456 holds the deposit until the remaining tenants deliver possession after a release under ORS 90.453 or an exclusion 'as provided in ORS 90.459 or 105.128' (ORS 90.456); it does not name a termination of a perpetrating tenant on 24 hours' written notice under ORS 90.445 where the perpetrator leaves without a court order. Because ORS 90.445(1)(a) keeps the other tenants' agreements in force, the row tells landlords to treat that case the same way (hold and account after the remaining tenants deliver possession), labelled Claude's reading; the alternative (a separate 31-day accounting to the perpetrator on departure) has no statutory footing either, and paying part of a joint deposit out early risks a claim from the remaining tenants. Also labelled Claude's reading: where the released victim was the only tenant, ORS 90.456 does not apply and ORS 90.300(12)-(13) run from the end of the tenancy and delivery of possession. The co-tenant division question is a confirmed absence (ORG3001); the row gives a cautious course (account to every paying tenant; refund as they agree in writing; take advice otherwise) rather than a rule.
- **dv-qualifying-documents**: The ORS 90.453(1)(b) definition of 'Qualified third party' includes 'an employee of the Department of Justice division providing victim and survivor services' (ORS 90.453), but the Part 2 role line of the statutory form in ORS 90.453(3) still lists only 'a law enforcement officer, attorney or licensed health professional or a victim's advocate with a victims services provider' (ORS 90.453). The row tells landlords to accept a statement signed by a DOJ victim-services employee because the statement need only be 'in substantially the following form' (ORS 90.453) and the definition controls who qualifies; rejecting it would risk a wrongful refusal of a release. Also: the row says ORS 90.325(4) damage verification carries no 90-day window, because ORS 90.325(4) borrows only the verification ('as provided by ORS 90.453' (ORS 90.325)) and the 90-day test sits in ORS 90.453(2)(c), which governs the release notice; labelled Claude's reading.
- **eviction-service-party**: No `eviction-service-party` clause is offered for Oregon. ORS 105.135(3) fixes service of an eviction summons at the premises (mailing to the defendant at the premises plus personal delivery or secure attachment at the main entrance), 'Notwithstanding ORCP 10' (ORS 105.135). A lease clause naming the tenant's agent could only add to that method, and a term that purported to substitute it would be a waiver of chapter 90 procedure that ORS 90.245(1)(a) makes unenforceable; the Oregon Rules of Civil Procedure are not in the corpus, so whether an appointed agent is a valid additional recipient was not checked. Offering a clause that adds nothing a landlord can rely on, while risking the knowing-use penalty if read as a waiver, isn't worth it. For an Address Confidentiality Program participant the row gives a cautious course (serve at the premises and also through the program), labelled Claude's reading, because neither ORS 105.135 nor ORS 192.820 to 192.868 reconciles the two.
- **cold-weather-vacate-notice**: Not tagging the shared clause `cold-weather-vacate-notice-wy` for Oregon (OR gaps log §4 class decision 17). Three reasons: (1) its written notice for absences of more than three days goes past what ORS 90.340 lets a rental agreement require ('any anticipated extended absence from the premises in excess of seven days' (ORS 90.340)), and ORS 90.410(1) attaches damages only to the seven-day notice 'as permitted by ORS 90.340'; whether a stricter contractual notice is enforceable is not settled by statute; (2) its charge for freeze damage the notice or heat "would have prevented" does not use the class decision 5 fault wording based on ORS 90.325(2)(b)-(3); (3) the tagged `extended-absence-notice-ks` already gives Oregon the statutory absence notice and ORS 90.410(2) gives entry during an absence over seven days. Oregon gets an education row only; no Oregon-specific clause is drafted because no Oregon statute calls for one.
- **trespass-notice**: Not tagging the shared clause `barred-person-covenant-ne` for Oregon. Its notes and drafting rest on Nebraska law (Neb. Rev. Stat. s 28-522(3), s 76-1431), including its domestic-violence carve-out, which tracks Nebraska's statute rather than ORS 90.449(3) (Oregon lets a landlord terminate a victim's tenancy only after a prior written warning and where the tenant permits a perpetrator who is 'an actual and imminent threat to the safety of persons on the premises other than the victim' (ORS 90.449(3)(a)) or lets the perpetrator live there as an unauthorized occupant). OR gaps log §4 class decision 17 bars tagging a row whose basis rests on another state's statute. Drafting an Oregon barred-person covenant would also need a reasonableness and ORS 90.262 rules analysis and a fair-housing screen, and no Oregon statute calls for one, so Oregon gets an education row only. If the main pass wants an Oregon clause, it should be optional, tied to conduct on the premises, and carve out every ORS 90.449 situation in Oregon's own words.
- **owner-move-in-reservation**: Not tagging the shared optional clause `owner-occupancy-termination-ar` for Oregon. It lets the landlord end a fixed Term early for owner or family occupancy on written notice; Oregon allows termination during a fixed term 'only for cause' (ORS 90.427(4)(a)), and the owner-occupancy reason applies to a fixed term only 'on or after the expiration of the fixed term' (ORS 90.427(5)), with 90 days' notice, a stated reason and supporting facts, and one month's rent unless the landlord owns an interest in four or fewer units (ORS 90.427(6)). A clause shortening those protections is a waiver ORS 90.245(1)(a) makes unenforceable, and knowing use risks the ORS 90.245(2) penalty and the ORS 90.427(9) liability for three months' rent. Oregon gets an education row; no clause.
- **automatic-renewal**: Not tagging the shared optional clause `automatic-renewal-ks` for Oregon, keeping OR log §18.2's call. Its renewal for a further fixed term on non-renewal notice of {{renewal_notice_days}} days, and its bar on ending a renewal term by 30-day notice, would bind the tenant to a new fixed term the tenant could otherwise avoid by written notice 'not less than 30 days prior to the specified ending date for the fixed term' (ORS 90.427(4)(c)(B)); whether a renewal agreed at signing counts as the parties' agreement 'to a new fixed term tenancy' (ORS 90.427(4)(c)(A)) is not settled by the text, and a term cutting into ORS 90.427 risks ORS 90.245(1)(a) and the knowing-use penalty. Its rent-change sentence (notice {{renewal_notice_days}} days before renewal) also does not track ORS 90.323's 90-day written notice, first-year bar and cap. Oregon gets an education row only.
- **utility-allowance-cap**: No Oregon allowance-plus-overage clause drafted. ORS 90.315(4)(a)-(c) already governs any charge a landlord passes to a tenant from a provider's bill, and `utility-billing-or` carries the required written terms (list of utilities, explanation of assessment and allocation, 30-day billing, copy of bill, cost-only). A landlord running an allowance plan can describe the allowance in that clause's allocation blank. A second clause would duplicate it and risk drifting from the ORS 90.315(4)(b) steps. Reading an overage as a 'utility or service charge' (ORS 90.315(4)(a)) is Claude's reading, stated as such in the row.
- **utility-disconnection-notice-authorization**: No Oregon clause offered requiring a tenant-customer to designate the landlord to receive shutoff notices. OAR 860-021-0017, OAR 860-036-1120 and OAR 860-037-0020 frame designation as an option the utility must offer its customer; no rule says whether a lease may compel it, so a compelled designation is left to the landlord's own advice (OR gaps log §4 class decision 19). The row tells the landlord a voluntary request is the cautious course. Status Present, because Oregon has the customer-designation rule the topic asks about, with the owner-authorization part absent.
- **meter-conservation-charge**: No lease clause drafted for the ORS 470.695 notice. The statute requires notice 'prior to any party signing the agreement' (ORS 470.695), so a clause inside the lease would be given only at signing; the row tells the landlord to give a separate written notice before signing. A separate pre-signing notice form (like the library's other outside-the-lease items, OR log §6.1) could be added later if Steinoak wants one. The 2026-10-04 log marked this topic "Not applicable: another state's charge"; ORS 470.695 shows Oregon has a rule on point, so the status changes to Present.
- **fire-code-standard**: The 2025 ORS-sections-affected table lists Or. Laws 2025, ch. 564 (HB 2342), § 8 as amending ORS 476.030. The enrolled chapter 564 in the corpus contains no reference to ORS chapter 476; its section 8 amends ORS 497.061 (wildlife fees). The compiled ORS 476.030 history ends "2025 c.177 §3" with no c.564 entry. Treated as a table misattribution and recorded in the row's notes; the main pass may wish to confirm against the Legislature's online table.
- **appliances-excluded**: No Oregon items-not-supplied clause drafted and `appliances-excluded-la` not tagged. The shared clause carves out only physical injury and intentional or gross fault, so it would limit liability for ordinary negligence, which ORS 90.245(1)(c) bars. Many states have their own appliances-excluded clause; an Oregon version (list of items left but not supplied, tenant may ask for removal, no exculpation, nothing that ORS 90.320(1) requires) is a candidate for the main pass, but gaps item 2 calls for short pointer rows here, so the row tells the landlord to list supplied items in `appliances-included` and remove the rest.
- **bed-bug-cooperation**: Neither `pest-cooperation-pr` nor `pest-cooperation-la` is tagged OR: each charges the tenant for an infestation caused by the fault of the tenant, a household member or a consenting guest, which is wider than ORS 90.325(2)(b) and has no ORS 90.325(3)(b) exception (OR gaps log §4 class decisions 5 and 17). No Oregon pest-cooperation clause was drafted in this slice: a charge for treatment raises the fee-versus-damages question under ORS 90.302 (class decision 4 says damages for breach are not fees, as Claude's reading) and the fault wording of class decision 5, and the row states both limits. Suggested for the main pass: an optional Oregon clause (prompt report of suspected pests; follow reasonable written preparation instructions; entry under this Lease's entry section per ORS 90.322; cost only for an infestation within the class decision 5 fault wording).
- **ordnance-demolition-meter-disclosures**: Status set to Present because one of the topic's three disclosures (the shared-meter disclosure, ORS 90.315(2)) is an Oregon requirement; the ordnance and pre-lease demolition parts are confirmed absent with ORG4027. The 2026-10-04 log called the whole topic "Not applicable: another state's disclosures"; the meter part is answered by `shared-utility-disclosure-or`.
- **lead-safe-certification**: Owner renovating its own rental (OR log §7 open item). Decision: the Construction Contractors Board licence for lead-based paint renovation (ORS 701.510(2); OAR 812-007-0300) most likely does not reach an owner, or the owner's employee, working on the owner's own rental, because a 'Contractor' under ORS 701.005(5)(a) acts 'for another' (ORS 701.005(5)(a)), ORS 701.010(7) exempts 'A person performing work on a property that person owns or performing work as the owner's employee, whether the property is occupied by the owner or not' (ORS 701.010(7)), and OAR 812-007-0300 excepts a contractor 'exempt from the board's licensing requirements' (OAR 812-007-0300). The Oregon Health Authority's firm certification (ORS 431A.358(2)) is left open because it turns on FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. § 745.83 (firm, renovation, for compensation) and FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. § 745.82, not held; the row gives the cautious course (use a certified firm and renovator) and is VERIFIED under OR gaps log §4 class decision 12. Reason: stating the CCB reading with its basis is more useful than leaving it open, and the cautious course protects the landlord whichever way the federal definition falls. When the main pass reads FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. § 745.82 and FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. § 745.83, it can replace the cautious sentence in `edu-no-lead-safe-certification-or` with the answer by erratum.
- **portfolio-thresholds**: Or. Laws 2026, ch. 64 (HB 4128) is a 2026 act outside ORS chapter 90 that class decision 10 does not list. It limits purchases of single-family residences by an 'Institutional real estate investor' (Or. Laws 2026, ch. 64, § 1(2)) owning 2,500 or more such residences, and the act prints 'Effective date January 1, 2027' (Or. Laws 2026, ch. 64). I cite it in `edu-portfolio-thresholds-or` (answering the topic's question on acquisition limits) and in `edu-no-single-family-zone-lease-limit-or` (as a buying limit, not a renting limit), as an uncompiled act with its compiled number pending. Reason: the topic reference asks this exact question (Michigan's 2026 act). Suggest a legal-watch item for compilation and for the January 1, 2027 effective date.
- **plain-language-consumer-statement**: ORS 180.540 to ORS 180.555 create a voluntary Department of Justice plain-language review for consumer contracts up to $50,000 (with OAR 137-020-0100). I read it as not reaching chapter 90 rental agreements, because ORS 180.540(3)(a) uses 'Real estate, goods or services as defined in ORS 646.605' (ORS 180.540(3)(a)) and ORS 646.605 says '“Real estate” does not cover conduct covered by ORS chapter 90' (ORS 646.605(6)(b)(A)). On that reading `edu-no-plain-language-rule-or` stays correct, so I filed no finding against it; the new row `edu-no-plain-language-consumer-statement-or` describes the program and labels the reading as Claude's. If the main pass reads it otherwise, `edu-no-plain-language-rule-or` would need a finding.
- **inspection-condemnation-disclosure**: Battery ORG5013 stated a claim (no facility condemnation-notice timing rule) that its own term B hits disproved: ORS 90.645(9) (parks) and ORS 90.671(7) (marinas) require the landlord to notify tenants 'no later than 15 days after the landlord receives notice of the exercise of eminent domain or of the agency order' (ORS 90.645(9)). I reran it as ORG5014 with the corrected claim and stated the facility rule in `edu-inspection-condemnation-disclosure-or` as a boundary note only (class decision 1). The topic's New Mexico question therefore has an Oregon facility counterpart (15 days).
- **animal-care-access**: No Oregon animal-care clause offered (unlike `animal-care-access-vi`, tagged in 18 states). Reason: ORS 90.322(5) says 'A landlord has no other right of access except' (ORS 90.322(5)) by court order, ORS 90.410(2) absence or abandonment, and ORS 90.322(1)(f) speaks of an agreement 'regarding a specific entry' (ORS 90.322(1)(f)), so a standing lease term letting Landlord admit a tenant-named caretaker whenever an animal is left without care would add an access right outside ORS 90.322 and risks ORS 90.245(1)(a). The cautious course is an education row: emergency entry (ORS 90.322(1)(b)), absence entry (ORS 90.410(2)), a specific-entry agreement at the time (ORS 90.322(1)(f)), peace-officer entry (ORS 167.345(2)) and ORS 90.425(7)(b)(B) after the tenancy. The main pass may decide whether a narrower clause (Tenant's designation of a caretaker as contact information only, with no entry right) is worth offering; any such clause must reckon with Or. Laws 2026, ch. 61, § 2 if it involves sharing a phone number.
- **emergency-contact**: No emergency-contact lease clause drafted. Collecting a contact needs no clause, and the consent that would let Landlord tell the contact about the tenant must be 'With the separate written consent of the individual' (Or. Laws 2026, ch. 61), so it cannot sit in the lease (consistent with `edu-tenant-confidential-information-or` and OR log §6.1, which lists the confidential-information consent as outside the lease by statute). Whether a third party's own phone number, supplied by the tenant, is 'confidential information relating to a tenant' (Or. Laws 2026, ch. 61) under § 2(2) is unsettled; the row takes the cautious course (treat it as confidential) and labels it Claude's reading. The death-contact designation stays in `tenant-death-contact-or` (ORS 90.425(21)(a)(C) asks only for a writing). Product note for the main pass: a separate consent form for emergency contacts would be a separate document, not a lease row.
- **ev-charging-end-of-tenancy**: No Oregon end-of-tenancy charger clause drafted. ORS 90.462(8) lets the parties 'negotiate a different outcome' (ORS 90.462(8)) on ownership, but that is a negotiation, not a term the statute conditions on what the rental agreement says (rule 27 does not compel a clause). A standard removal-and-restoration clause would add a duty ORS 90.462 does not impose on residential tenants (contrast ORS 91.265(7)(c) for commercial tenants) and, if it charged Tenant for restoration, would have to reconcile ORS 90.462(6)(b)'s no-fault damage rule with class decision 5's fault wording. The education row records the lawful option (class decision 19). The Colorado-based `ev-charging-end-of-tenancy-co` and `ev-charging-shared-area-co` remain untagged (OR notes of `edu-ev-charging-or`).
- **ev-charging-requirements**: Weights and measures: ORS 618.121 licenses only devices 'specified in ORS 618.141' (ORS 618.121), and that list has no electricity meter, so the row says Oregon has no charger registration; but ORS 618.010(17) defines 'Weights and measures' broadly and excludes only public-utility meters, so whether chapter 618's general accuracy provisions reach a landlord's kilowatt-hour billing meter is left open with the cautious course (ask the State Department of Agriculture). Chapter 618's accuracy sections were not read whole for this row; the main pass may read ORS 618.010 to 618.246 if it wants the point settled.
- **furnishings-included**: Drafted Oregon's own optional furnishings clause (`furnishings-included-or`) instead of tagging `furnishings-included-la`. The shared clause requires Tenant to "return them at the end of this Lease in the same condition, except for normal wear and tear" (clause text), a strict condition duty that would make Tenant answer for damage from acts of God or a perpetrator's conduct, which ORS 90.325(3) excludes; class decision 5 requires the fault wording for any clause charging Tenant for damage. The Oregon clause keeps the shared structure (inventory, keep at the property, no removal, prompt notice) and states the charge with the class decision 5 wording, excluding ordinary wear and tear as ORS 90.300(7)(a)(B) does. The OR log §18.2 status (answered by `appliances-included`) is superseded because appliances and furniture are different subjects.
- **prohibited-acts-renter**: Tagged the shared clause `prohibited-acts-renter-wy` (24 states) instead of drafting an Oregon clause. Its three covenants (no deliberate or negligent damage or knowingly permitting it; no interference with others' peaceful enjoyment; no unreasonable refusal of entry for repairs, inspection or showing) match ORS 90.325(2)(b), ORS 90.325(1)(g) and ORS 90.322(2) in substance; it charges nothing (ORS 90.302 screen) and exculpates no one (ORS 90.245(1)(c) screen); its basis (SERVES_LANDLORD) rests on no other state's statute for Oregon (class decision 17). It omits Oregon's alarm and sprinkler bars (ORS 90.325(2)(a), (c)); those are stated in the companion education row and already carried by `alarm-duties-or`-type rows, so no Oregon variant was drafted. The OR log §18.2 pointer to `edu-tenant-statutory-duties-or` is honored by the companion row `edu-prohibited-acts-renter-or`.
- **farmland-hunting-rights**: Answered as Present (not Absent) because ORS 498.120(1) speaks to who may permit hunting on cultivated or enclosed land ('the owner or lawful occupant thereof' (ORS 498.120(1))), which answers the South Dakota question; the Arkansas-style owner-reservation rule is recorded as absent with batteries ORG6011 (recorded as failed, rerun as ORG6012). Claude's readings, labeled in the row: a tenant in possession is a "lawful occupant" able to permit hunting, and a farmland lease's hunting terms fall outside ORS chapter 90 except for any dwelling unit rented with the land. No clause offered: hunting reservations are a farm-lease term outside the residential library.
- **binding-arbitration**: Gaps item 10 (arbitration): not offered, no tag of `arbitration-agreement`. ORS 36.620(1) validates arbitration agreements generally, but a chapter 90 rental agreement may not waive chapter 90 remedies (ORS 90.245(1)(a)), the only carve-out (ORS 90.220(5)) excludes 'mandatory mediation or arbitration' (ORS 90.100(24)), and ORS 90.245(2) attaches a penalty of up to three months' periodic rent to deliberately using and attempting to enforce a known prohibited term. A penalty attaches and the answer turns on case law (and on FAA preemption, FEDERAL: 9 U.S.C. § 2, unread case law), so under Taylor's 2026-11-07 decision the library offers no clause; `edu-binding-arbitration-or` says so. Not asked of Taylor (the prompt settles it).
- **(item 8) smoking-policy**: `smoking-policy` is still not tagged OR; Oregon uses its `smoking-policy-or` choice group (default `smoking-policy-or`, `smoking-allowed-or`, `smoking-limited-areas-or`). The three bodies match the statute's three categories ('The disclosure must state whether smoking is prohibited on the premises, allowed on the entire premises or allowed in limited areas on the premises' (ORS 479.305(1))), the limited-areas version identifies the areas, and the rows' notes already state Or. Laws 2026, ch. 60 (the new 'smoking' definition in ORS 479.305(1), effective January 1, 2027 per the enrolled act's filing line, which matches ORS 171.022 for an act with no effective-date section; applied to existing agreements by § 3) and the ORS 90.262(2)(b) mid-tenancy indoor-ban exception (`edu-smoking-or`). Confirmed; no change.
- **(item 8) no-alterations**: `no-alterations` is still not tagged OR; `no-alterations-or` fits ORS 90.462(8), ORS 90.265(1) and ORS 90.355(2)-(3), with one proposed clarification for disability modifications (finding on `no-alterations-or`). `political-access` and `religious-cultural-display` are answered in slice F.
- **(item 8) addendum-precedence**: `addendum-precedence` (carried by OR) does not make the Lease control over a separate writing Oregon law requires or protects. The smoking disclosure is required in the rental agreement (ORS 90.220(4); ORS 479.305(1)), so it controls under the clause's own exception. The other writings are not "addenda attached to this Lease" at signing: the e-mail notice addendum must be 'executed by both parties after the tenancy begins and the tenant has occupied the premises' (ORS 90.155(1)(d)(C)) and so amends the Lease as a later signed writing (`entire-agreement`), and `notices` defers to the statute's methods rather than barring e-mail; the showing agreement must be 'reflected in a writing separate from the rental agreement and signed by both parties' (ORS 90.322(1)(d)(B)); the temporary occupancy agreement is a three-party writing (ORS 90.275(1)); the chores writing is the Lease's own `tenant-maintenance-tasks-or` paragraph or a separately signed writing meeting ORS 90.320(2). Confirmed; no change.
- **(item 8) default-by-tenant-or, keys-or, hoa-compliance-or**: `default-by-tenant-or` fits ORS 90.302 and ORS 90.401: remedies are those "applicable law provides" (clause text), no fee or cost-shifting beyond ORS 90.255, and it states ORS 90.302(6)'s rule that a fee is not rent. `keys-or` fits: it charges only for replacing a key lost by Tenant at Tenant's request (ORS 90.302(7)(c)) and claims re-keying for unreturned keys as damages or from the deposit (ORS 90.401(2); ORS 90.300(7)(a)(A)). `hoa-compliance-or` fits: association fines are recovered as damages for Tenant's breach (ORS 90.401(2)), and move-in and move-out assessments meet ORS 90.302(7)(f)(B)-(D) (stated in the Lease; copy given before or at the time of the charge). `parking-tag-towing-or` states no tag price, so the 2026-11-12 parking-tag decision is not engaged. No change proposed.
- **(item 9b) tenant-caused-damage-or**: Casualty fault persons: Oregon has no casualty clause (`edu-casualty-termination-or`). `tenant-caused-damage-or` names Tenant and "a person on the premises with Tenant's permission or consent" (clause text), which matches the tenant-remedy fault bars word for word in substance: 'The tenant may not terminate or recover damages under this section for a condition caused by the deliberate or negligent act or omission of the tenant or other person on the premises with the tenant’s permission or consent' (ORS 90.360(4)); 'Rights of the tenant under this section do not arise if the condition was caused by the deliberate or negligent act or omission of the tenant or a person on the premises with the tenant’s consent' (ORS 90.365(6)), and ORS 90.368(5)(d) is to the same effect. It excludes acts of God and a perpetrator's conduct (ORS 90.325(3)), as ORS 90.453 to 90.459 require. Two statutes use narrower fault words, and the clause defers to both through "except as applicable law provides": the tenant's own duty reaches damage the tenant does 'or knowingly permit any person to do so' (ORS 90.325(2)(b)), so the repair-cost sentence rests on contract for a guest's damage Tenant did not knowingly permit (a lawful term, ORS 90.220(1); recoverable from the deposit as a default 'in the performance of the rental agreement' (ORS 90.300(7)(a)(A))); and a tenant may terminate a posted unit if the conditions 'Were not caused by the tenant' (ORS 90.380(5)(a)), which a guest's fault does not defeat. The disaster rules excuse rent with no fault exception ('A tenant does not owe rent while the dwelling unit is inaccessible due to the natural disaster or the destruction of the dwelling unit' (Or. Laws 2026, ch. 108, § 21(3))) and relieve cleanup 'Unless a tenant is responsible for the natural disaster' (Or. Laws 2026, ch. 108, § 21(1)(b)); the clause's rent sentence names those rules. The fault persons match the statutes the clause relies on; no wording proposed. New charge clauses in this pass use OR gaps log §4 class decision 5 (the ORS 90.325(2)(b) and (3) wording).
- **(item 9c) CARES**: None of `edu-nonpayment-notice-or`, `edu-cure-and-eviction-grounds-or`, `edu-termination-notice-or`, `edu-for-cause-eviction-or`, `edu-expedited-criminal-eviction-or`, `edu-eviction-process-or`, `default-by-tenant-or`, `criminal-activity-or`, `holdover-or` or `surrender-end-of-term-or` contradicts `edu-cares-act-notice` (tagged OR). Each states Oregon's notice periods as the notices Oregon requires; none says no other notice is needed, and `default-by-tenant-or` and `holdover-or` act only "as applicable law" allows (clause text). For a covered property the federal 30-day notice to vacate runs on top of Oregon's shorter notices (FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c)), as the federal row says.
- **(item 11) OAR 333-062-1000**: As served November 18, 2026, OAR 333-062-1000 carries 'Temporary rule language in effect until 12/18/2026' (OAR 333-062-1000) and its History line reads "PH 26-2026, temporary amend filed 06/18/2026, effective 06/22/2026 through 12/18/2026" after "PH 9-2025, adopt filed 04/01/2025, effective 04/01/2025" (history line); no permanent amendment is shown. The date has not passed, and `edu-pool-rules-or` already states the temporary period. Nothing to report now; the legal-watch item "or-2026-12-18-oar-333-062" checks the rule after December 18, 2026. A live re-check on 2026-11-19 was not possible: secure.sos.state.or.us answered an in-page request with a bot-challenge script, so the pass stopped using that site (prompt: don't work around a block). The corpus copy and 82 live rule texts compared earlier the same day match (OR gaps log §1).
- **appliances-excluded**: Main pass: the slice D suggestion of an Oregon items-not-supplied clause is declined. The carrying clause `appliances-included` already lists what you supply, and the habitability duty reaches 'Ventilating, air conditioning and other facilities and appliances, including elevators, maintained in good repair if supplied or required to be supplied by the landlord' (ORS 90.320(1)(i)), so it turns on what you supply; a second clause would add nothing a complete list does not, and any disclaimer wording risks the exculpation bar (ORS 90.245(1)(c)). `edu-appliances-excluded-or` states the rule and points to `appliances-included` (OR gaps log §4 class decision 19).
- **bed-bug-cooperation**: Main pass: the slice D suggestion of an optional Oregon pest-cooperation clause is declined for this pass. The cooperation parts (report, prepare, allow entry) restate duties the lease and ORS 90.322 already carry, and the charge part would have to survive ORS 90.302 (a closed fee list) and use class decision 5's fault wording; no Oregon statute or rule on bed bugs or pest treatment in rentals was found (`edu-no-bed-bug-rule-or`), so there is no statutory condition that only lease text satisfies (rule 27). `edu-bed-bug-cooperation-or` states both limits (OR gaps log §4 class decision 19).

#### 4.3 New `{{variables}}`

None.

### 5. Independent check

#### 5.1 Round 1 (all new rows, tags and findings)
- **Scope:** 173 objects (165 rows, 2 tags, 6 findings on existing rows), checked in five parts of 33–35 objects, one checker at a time. Each checker had no part in drafting and judged the rows against the corpus text with the section printer, the battery tools and the quote checker.
- **Results:** 6 ERROR, 131 FIX, 47 NOTE; 77 objects OK with no finding. I applied 137 corrections as errata (seq 10100001 onward), and 4 main-pass errata (seq 9000003–9000006) narrowed the notes of the ERROR rows. The NOTEs were optional and were not applied.
- **The six ERRORs:** each one was an absence or rule stated more broadly than the law on disk allows:
  - `edu-exculpatory-clauses-or`: ORS 90.396(5) and ORS 105.580(3) protect a landlord acting in good faith against drug or criminal activity.
  - `edu-no-inspection-notice-penalty-or`: an inspection during possession is an entry under ORS 90.322(1)(f), with the ORS 90.322(8) remedy.
  - `edu-no-rent-demand-bar-or`: retaliation is no bar to possession where the tenant was in default in rent (ORS 90.385(4)(c), (6)).
  - `edu-forfeiture-redemption-or`: rent ends when the termination notice expires (ORS 90.220(8)(a)); the row had borrowed the abandonment rule.
  - `edu-no-usufructuary-lessor-rule-or`: trustees and conservators have statutory leasing powers (ORS 130.725(9); ORS 125.445(11)).
  - `edu-law-enforcement-cooperation-or`: the nuisance-abatement law ORS 105.550 to ORS 105.600 reaches rentals.

  In each case the row's battery had not reached the contradicting section. The battery and its count stand, and the row's notes now say what the battery supports.
- **Recurring FIX shapes:**
  - the ORS 90.245(2) penalty stated without its knowing-use condition;
  - the ORS 90.302(8) penalty stated without its subsection (2) exception;
  - tenant fault and knowledge bars cut short (ORS 90.360(2); ORS 90.365(6); ORS 90.325(2)(b), (3));
  - quotations cut after an opening "Except";
  - lists not read to the end;
  - 2026 acts not stated (Or. Laws 2026, ch. 108, § 21(3); ch. 60, § 2; ch. 23, § 3);
  - facility scope missing;
  - backtick pointers naming a topic key instead of a row id.
- **Reproducibility:** checkers re-ran the cited batteries and found no count that fails to re-run. The full replay (`orq.py replay`, two shards) re-ran all 191 logged batteries, 1,592 term runs, with 0 differences.

#### 5.2 Round 2 (rows edited after round 1)

- **Results:** 0 ERROR, 35 FIX, 8 NOTE, 49 OK.
- **Round-1 findings:** none was judged wrong. All six round-1 ERRORs were confirmed.
- **What the FIXes caught:** defects the round-1 edits had introduced:
  - qualifiers dropped from new restatements;
  - unchanged "found none" sentences now sitting after a rule the edit added;
  - restatements broader or narrower than the statute;
  - one missing comma.

  I applied all 35 as errata (seq 20100001 onward), after reading each replacement against the section it cites.
- **NOTEs not applied, recorded for the sync:**
  - Or. Laws 2025, ch. 476, § 8 (effective July 17, 2025) appears in tension with the compiled ORS 93.277(2)(a) cited in `edu-no-single-family-zone-lease-limit-or`. Legal Counsel's compilation controls the text quoted; worth a legal-watch look.
  - The proposed wording in the finding on `edu-lead-based-paint-or` should keep the painted-surface and pre-1978 scope when synced.

### 6. Findings on existing rows

- **`edu-required-fees-or`**: Completeness, not an error: the row's list of charges outside the fee rules ("Outside the fee rules: attorney fees awarded by a court, screening charges, charges for improvements or other actions the tenant asks for ... and association assessments passed through as the statute allows") follows ORS 90.302(7) and omits the one statutory override elsewhere in the chapter: 'Notwithstanding ORS 90.302, a landlord may charge a tenant for filing fees paid under ORS 105.130, if the complaint for possession is dismissed under subsection (3)(c) of this section' (ORS 90.395), that is, when rental assistance or another payment covering the amount owed is tendered; 'Payment of the fees is not a prerequisite for dismissal under subsection (3)(c) of this section' (ORS 90.395). It is stated in edu-nonpayment-notice-or ("Filing fees: ORS 90.395(5)"), so at sync the main pass may add a short clause to edu-required-fees-or's list, for example: "and, if a nonpayment eviction is dismissed because rental assistance or another payment covering the amount owed was tendered, the court filing fee you paid (paying it is not a condition of the dismissal)." ORS 90.395 read whole (corpus-OR-20261118; history 2023 c.13 §55; no 2025 or 2026 amendment listed).
- **`edu-eviction-process-or`**: The row's notes say "Court rules (UTCR and supplementary local rules) were not loaded: OR log §7 (open item, rule 39 boundary)", and the body ends "Some counties' courts have their own forms and procedures." The 2026 UTCR are now in the corpus (effective August 1, 2026 under CJO 26-021) and add eviction-specific court rules the row doesn't state: 'In the caption of a complaint filed in an eviction action, the plaintiff must indicate if the case is subject to ORS chapter 90 in substantially the following form' (UTCR 18.020), with the box "[ ] Subject to the Residential Landlord and Tenant Act."; 'A complaint in an eviction action that is subject to ORS chapter 90 must be in substantially the form provided at www.courts.oregon.gov/forms' (UTCR 18.030(2)); any proposed order or judgment 'must include a finding as to whether the action is subject to ORS chapter 90' (UTCR 18.040(1)), a judgment naming several defendants 'must clearly describe the judgment rendered as to each defendant' (UTCR 18.040(2)), and a chapter 90 FED judgment 'must be in substantially the form provided at www.courts.oregon.gov/forms' (UTCR 18.040(3)). Also, 'Upon notice that proceedings in an action are subject to a federal bankruptcy stay, the court must stay the action' (UTCR 7.050(1)) (see the new `edu-tenant-bankruptcy-or`). Proposed body sentence for the sync, replacing the last sentence: "Under the 2026 court rules, your complaint's caption must say whether the case is subject to the Residential Landlord and Tenant Act, the complaint and judgment must follow the statewide forms on the Oregon Judicial Department's website, and a judgment against several tenants must state the result for each (UTCR 18.020 to UTCR 18.040, court rules); some counties also have supplementary local rules." Proposed notes addition: "UTCR 18.010 to UTCR 18.040 and UTCR 7.050 read (2026 UTCR, effective August 1, 2026, CJO 26-021); county supplementary local rules not held."
- **`edu-lead-based-paint-or`**: The row says a contractor doing lead-based paint renovation must be a Construction Contractors Board certified lead-based paint renovation contractor, and adds in parentheses "if you do such work yourself, check whether the Board's rules reach you" (row text). It omits the separate Oregon Health Authority firm certification: 'A firm may not perform or offer to perform a renovation for compensation unless the firm is certified as provided under ORS 431A.355' (ORS 431A.358(2)), with civil penalties of 'not more than $5,000 per violation per day' (ORS 431A.363(1)) and a remediation order available against 'A property owner, or agent of the property owner, who knowingly contracted with a person who was not certified' (ORS 431A.363(3)(a)(B)). Proposed wording for the second sentence: "Oregon adds no lease disclosure, but renovation for compensation (as federal rules define renovation) must be done by a firm certified by the Oregon Health Authority and, for contractors, by a Construction Contractors Board certified lead-based paint renovation contractor (see `edu-no-lead-safe-certification-or` for whether this reaches work you do yourself)." Notes addition: ORS 431A.353; ORS 431A.358(2); ORS 431A.363(1), (3). The new rows `edu-lead-state-notices-or` and `edu-no-lead-safe-certification-or` carry the detail, so the row could instead simply point to them.
- **`no-alterations-or`**: Gaps item 8 (`no-alterations`). `no-alterations-or` says an approved alteration "will remain part of the property at the end of the tenancy unless Landlord requires its removal or applicable law provides that it remains Tenant's property". For a reasonable modification made for a disability, the landlord's power to require restoration is limited: 'However, in the case of a rental, the landlord may, when it is reasonable to do so, condition permission for a reasonable modification on the renter agreeing to restore the interior of the premises to the condition that existed before the modification, reasonable wear and tear excepted' (ORS 659A.145(2)(f)); the federal rule is the same ('In the case of a rental, the landlord may, where it is reasonable to do so, condition permission for a modification on the renter agreeing to restore the interior of the premises to the condition that existed before the modification, reasonable wear and tear excepted' (FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.203(a))). The row's last sentence protects the modification itself ("does not limit any ... reasonable modification that applicable law entitles Tenant to perform") but not the restoration limit, so the clause can be read to let Landlord require removal of, for example, a widened doorway, which Example (2) of FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.203(c) says a landlord 'may not, in usual circumstances' require (FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.203(c)). Proposed wording (rule 80 erratum form): old "unless Landlord requires its removal or applicable law provides" new "unless Landlord requires its removal (for a reasonable modification made for a disability, only where applicable law allows Landlord to require restoration) or applicable law provides". The rest of the row fits: portable cooling devices are preserved by the last sentence and are restricted only as ORS 90.355(2) allows; an alternative energy device needs written permission and 'is not a fixture in which the landlord has a legal interest, except as otherwise expressly provided in a written agreement between the landlord and tenant' (ORS 90.265(1)), which the clause's deference to law keeps with the tenant; the EV station rule (ORS 90.462(8)) is named.
- **`early-termination-or`**: Gaps item 9(a). The fee attaches only to Tenant's contractual early exit, and the last sentence keeps every statutory termination, so the clause does not charge a fee on the terminations ORS 90.360(1), ORS 90.365, ORS 90.380, ORS 90.472, ORS 90.475 and Or. Laws 2026, ch. 108, § 21 give (the statute allows the fee only for 'The abandonment or relinquishment of a dwelling unit during a fixed term tenancy without cause' (ORS 90.302(2)(e)), and a natural-disaster destruction ends the tenancy by law: 'the tenancy is immediately terminated and the parties are not further obligated under the rental agreement or this chapter' (Or. Laws 2026, ch. 108, § 21(1); uncompiled, added to ORS 90.100 to 90.465 by § 20; effective June 5, 2026, § 31)). Two gaps in the stated no-fee list: (1) it names only "Oregon's protections" for servicemembers, but federal law reaches cases ORS 90.472 and ORS 90.475 don't (a lease signed before entering service, a permanent change of station or a deployment of not less than 90 days, a stop-movement order, and termination by a spouse or dependent after the servicemember's death or catastrophic injury or illness; FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(a)(3), (a)(4), (b)(1)), and in each 'The lessor may not impose an early termination charge' (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(e)(1)); (2) it does not say the fee is never charged on the other statutory terminations, which a landlord reading the clause could miss. Proposed wording: old "Landlord will not charge the fee if Tenant ends the tenancy under Oregon's protections for victims of domestic violence, sexual assault, bias crime or stalking or for servicemembers, or after Landlord has given notice ending the tenancy at the end of the Term for a landlord's qualifying reason." new "Landlord will not charge the fee if Tenant ends the tenancy under the protections Oregon or federal law gives victims of domestic violence, sexual assault, bias crime or stalking, or servicemembers and their spouses or dependents; after Landlord has given notice ending the tenancy at the end of the Term for a landlord's qualifying reason; or under any other right applicable law gives Tenant to end the tenancy." `rule_type` stays RECOMMENDED (Taylor, 2026-11-12).
- **`edu-servicemember-rights-or`**: Gaps item 9(a). The row states ORS 90.475 and ORS 90.472 and says the termination date is the earlier of "any date federal law sets" (row text) and the state dates, but it does not say that federal law also gives termination rights Oregon's list doesn't: the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act covers 'A lease of premises occupied, or intended to be occupied, by a servicemember or a servicemember’s dependents for a residential, professional, business, agricultural, or similar purpose' where 'the lease is executed by or on behalf of a person who thereafter and during the term of the lease enters military service' (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(b)(1)(A)) or the servicemember in service later 'receives military orders for a permanent change of station or to deploy with a military unit, or as an individual in support of a military operation, for a period of not less than 90 days' (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(b)(1)(B)), with no "outside the area" condition of the kind ORS 90.475(1)(b) sets; a spouse or dependent may terminate 'during the one-year period beginning on the date of the death of the lessee' (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(a)(3)); and 'The lessor may not impose an early termination charge' (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(e)(1)). Proposed added sentence: "Federal law adds cases Oregon's statute doesn't list: under the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act a servicemember may end a residential lease signed before entering military service, or signed while in service and followed by orders for a permanent change of station or a deployment of 90 days or more, or signed on such orders and followed by a stop-movement order, indefinite or for 30 days or more, that prevents occupancy; a spouse or dependent may end it within one year after the servicemember dies in service; and within one year after a catastrophic injury or illness in service the servicemember (or, if the servicemember lacks mental capacity, a spouse or dependent) may end it; you may not charge an early termination charge in any of these cases (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955)." No library row carrying OR states the federal rule (searched by section number 3955).
- **`edu-rent-receipts-or`**: Completeness, not an error. The row states the payment-writing rule of ORS 90.140(2) for every landlord but not the separate cash rule for licensed property managers: 'A property manager must prepare a legible written receipt for any cash funds received under a property management agreement' (OAR 863-025-0060(1)), on consecutively pre-numbered forms in at least duplicate, with a copy kept (OAR 863-025-0060(3)-(4)). Proposed body addition for the sync: "If a licensed property manager collects rent in cash for you, the manager must also write a numbered receipt for it and keep a copy (OAR 863-025-0060)." Found by battery ORG6 term C (superseded by ORG7 for its claim; `edu-confirmed-absences-outside-title-or`).

### 7. Findings for other states

None.

### Proposed SOP changes

1. Rule 60 (helper canvass): each helper brief should name a private scratch folder (for example slices/<X>/tmp/). In the Oregon gaps pass two helpers wrote same-named scripts to the shared scratchpad, and one save silently used the other's script; the helper caught it on re-check and nothing was lost.

### Proposed topic questions

1. `notice-to-quit-waiver`: Does an older general-tenancy statute let a written lease waive the notice to quit, and does the residential landlord-tenant act displace it for dwellings it covers? (Oregon: ORS 105.120(2) and ORS 91.100 allow the waiver, but ORS 90.120(1) makes ORS 91.010 to ORS 91.110 inapplicable to chapter 90 tenancies and ORS 105.120(3) governs chapter 90 units.)
2. `exculpatory-clauses`: Does a statute give the landlord immunity, with no lease clause needed, for equipment the tenant installs, such as a portable cooling device? (Oregon: ORS 90.355(4).)
3. `part5-nonwaivable`: Does a foreclosure-rescue or equity-purchaser statute void a waiver in a sale-and-lease-back of a home in foreclosure, even where no recent act exists? (Oregon: ORS 646A.735(2)(a), enacted 2008.)
4. `showing-notice-waiver`: If the state allows a no-notice showing agreement, is it limited to prospective buyers (not prospective tenants), and must it be signed while the landlord is actively trying to sell? (Oregon: ORS 90.322(1)(d)(A)-(C).)
5. `dv-qualifying-documents`: Where a state's statutory third-party verification form lags an amended definition of who may sign it (for example a new category of signer added to the definition but not to the form's role line), which controls, and should a landlord accept a statement from the newly added signer?
6. `tenant-bankruptcy`: Does a state court rule (rather than a statute) require the trial court to stay an action, including an eviction, once it has notice of a federal bankruptcy stay, and does it bar dismissal solely because of the bankruptcy filing? (Oregon: UTCR 7.050(1), (3).)
7. `accessory-dwelling-unit`: Does state law relax eviction or rent rules when the landlord lives on a small property with an accessory unit (for example, no for-cause requirement after the first year where the unit is on the same property as the landlord's primary residence and the property has no more than two dwelling units)? (Oregon: ORS 90.427(8).)
8. `tenant-employee-housing`: Does the state give visitors (government officials, health and service providers, invited guests) a right of access to employer-provided housing, and bar evicting the employee for complaints? (Oregon: ORS 659A.250 to ORS 659A.262, all employer housing, not only farm housing.)
9. `association-obligations-disclosure`: Does the condominium act treat a developer's long lease (Oregon: more than three years including renewals) as a sale, so the lessee gets the public disclosure statement and the declaration and bylaws? (Oregon: ORS 100.005(32); ORS 100.705(2); ORS 100.725(1).)
10. `animal-care-access`: Does the abandoned-property statute say what a landlord may or must do with pets or livestock left behind (call animal control, give them to a shelter, a duty of reasonable care)? (Oregon: ORS 90.425(7)(b)(B).)
11. `purpose-limitation`: Does a general tenancy statute outside the residential act void a rental made for, or let the lessor avoid a lease after, a gambling or other unlawful use, and how does its remedy fit the residential eviction procedure? (Oregon: ORS 91.240(2)-(3).)
12. `law-enforcement-cooperation`: Does the residential act protect a landlord from penalties under a local nuisance or crime ordinance that requires removing a tenant, when the landlord's good-faith eviction fails in court? (Oregon: ORS 90.396(5).)
13. `ev-charging-requirements`: Does the weights-and-measures device-license schedule list electricity meters or chargers at all, and does the definition of weights and measures exclude only public-utility meters? (Oregon: ORS 618.010(17), ORS 618.121, ORS 618.141.) Does the cash-acceptance law exempt electric vehicle charging stations? (Oregon: ORS 659A.410(3)(h).)

## Federal row: disposing of screening reports (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20 (Washington gaps log §7, backlog item 9): one federal education row, `edu-fcra-disposal-rule`, is tagged here and in every jurisdiction, read from the official text (16 C.F.R. part 682 on eCFR; 15 U.S.C. § 1681w on govinfo.gov). It says that anyone who keeps a screening, credit or background report, or a record taken from one, for a business purpose must dispose of it with reasonable measures against unauthorized access (16 C.F.R. § 682.3), with the rule's examples, and that the rule sets no retention period (§ 682.4). This state's existing rows stay; no clause text changed.

## Bracketed prompts cleared (Claude Code, 2026-11-27)

Taylor approved. Every bracket in this state's own clause text was handled as in Colorado's pilot: formatting moved to `docs/pdf-formatting.md`, a note to the landlord moved to the clause's "Use when" line, its education or a field's help, and a blank or choice became a `{{variable}}` asked under the clause. No lease term changed. Bracketed prompts cleared 2026-11-27 (26 clauses). Formatting moved out of the text: the foreclosure disclosure and the well-water notice are given in writing before signing (ORS 90.310(1), ORS 90.321(8)). Stored data now fills the returned-payment fee, amounts due at signing, other payment methods, the tenant's tasks and what the tenant gets for them, the landlord's notice address, the manager and service-of-process recipient (existing choices), the utility allocation method and payment days, alarm testing instructions, the alarm tampering fee, the death contact, the designated repairer and the furnishings inventory. New questions under the clause: whether the tenant pays an electronic processing fee, the cure-payment location, the rule-violation fee kinds (six combinations of the seven ORS 90.302(3)(b) kinds) and three fee amounts, the landlord's yard work, the child care requirement (release or bond), whether renter's liability insurance is required, the cooling restrictions and community cooling space, the tow company and guest parking, the notice drop location, the actual-notice method, the smoking areas, pending proceedings, shared tenant-paid utilities, the billed utilities and how they are assessed, a cable or internet markup, how utility bills are delivered, the recycling location and instructions, and well test results and next test dates. Limits read on oregonlegislature.gov (ORS chapters 30 and 90, 2025 edition): dishonored check fee $35 (ORS 90.302(2)(b), 30.701(5)), second rule-violation fee $50 and smoking or pet fee $250 (ORS 90.302(3)), alarm tampering fee $250 (ORS 90.302(2)(c)), cable or internet markup 10% (ORS 90.315(4)(c)(B)) and at least 30 days to pay a utility bill (ORS 90.315(4)(b)(D)). The later-violation cap ($50 plus 5% of the period's rent) and the renter's insurance cap ($100,000 or the customary amount, whichever is greater) depend on other figures, so they are help only. Nothing held.
